Continuing work from thread T-019c726b-c3fd-72c8-be61-06cdc86e123b. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@src/bin/screenshot.rs @src/view/mod.rs @src/model/ui.rs @website/src/data/showcase.ts @screenshots/scenarios/showcase-selection.yaml @screenshots/scenarios/showcase-command-palette.yaml @Cargo.toml

Other relevant files: src/model/editor.rs, website/src/pages/showcase.astro

I have been expanding the website and the automated screenshot infrastructure.

I implemented two new pages for the website:
- /showcase: A gallery of editor screenshots with tag filtering and a lightbox.
- /built-with-ai: A page detailing the development process using AI assistants, based on BUILDING_WITH_AI.md.

I significantly enhanced the screenshot tool in src/bin/screenshot.rs to support:
- Workspace/sidebar rendering by initializing a real Workspace model.
- Modal rendering (Command Palette, Find/Replace, Theme Picker, Goto Line) by making Renderer::render_modals public and adding ModalConfig to the YAML schema.
- Explicit text selections (SelectionRange) in the YAML schema to showcase multi-cursor highlights.

I registered the screenshot and profile_render binaries in Cargo.toml since autobins is set to false.

Current tasks and open issues:
- The selection screenshot (showcase-selection.yaml) looks poor because the blue overlay desaturates syntax colors and lacks definition. I need to rethink this to show word-level selections or more impactful ranges.
- The command palette screenshot should be improved to show the theme selection modal with a specific theme selected for variety.
- The screenshot tool currently only supports one split direction per scenario (all horizontal or all vertical). I want to expand it to support mixed/nested splits.
- I want to explore more interesting scenarios, such as pre-filled search in the command palette or specific workspace configurations.
- I need to ensure the file browser and workspace are showcased in various configurations.

continue

```
### Current State of Work

#### **Completed Work**
- **Website Pages**: Created `/showcase` (screenshot gallery with tag filtering) and `/built-with-ai` (methodology and development timeline).
- **Navigation**: Updated `Navbar.astro` and `SiteLayout.astro` to include links to the new pages.
- **Infrastructure**:
  - Registered `screenshot` and `profile_render` binaries in `Cargo.toml`.
  - Made `Renderer::render_modals` public in `src/view/mod.rs` to allow headless rendering.
- **Screenshot Tool Enhancements**: Updated `src/bin/screenshot.rs` to support:
  - **Workspace/Sidebar**: Configurable root, visibility, width, and expanded/selected nodes.
  - **Modals**: Support for `CommandPalette`, `GotoLine`, `FindReplace`, and `ThemePicker` with pre-filled inputs and selection indices.
  - **Selections**: Support for explicit `SelectionRange` (anchor/head) to highlight text blocks.

#### **Selection Screenshot Quality Issues**
Initial feedback on the selection screenshots indicated they were "poor" for several reasons:
- **Low Contrast**: The semi-transparent blue/indigo overlay was difficult to see against the dark background.
- **Muddied Syntax**: The overlay desaturated the underlying syntax colors, making the code look "dirty."
- **Lack of Definition**: The block/column selection looked like a flat alpha-mask without borders or glow.
- **Visual Weight**: The white cursor bar was too high-contrast compared to the selection itself, drawing the eye away from the selected content.

#### **Command Palette Improvements**
The user requested more variety for the command palette showcase. Instead of a simple search, the scenario was updated to showcase the **Theme Picker** modal with a specific theme selected to demonstrate the UI's interactive list and checkmark system.

#### **In-Progress Rework**
To address the quality issues, the following scenarios were updated and re-rendered:
- **`showcase-selection.yaml`**: Reworked to simulate a "Select Next Match" (`Cmd+J`) workflow. It now highlights the word `name` across multiple lines (10, 17, 19, and 39) in `sample.rs` to provide a more compelling visual of multi-cursor capability.
- **`showcase-command-palette.yaml`**: Updated to use `id: theme_picker` with `selected_index: 5` to show variety in modal types.

#### **Identified Gaps & Next Steps**
1. **Mixed/Nested Splits**: The screenshot tool currently only supports a single split direction (all horizontal or all vertical). The next step is to implement a recursive layout tree in the YAML schema to support complex layouts (e.g., a horizontal split where one side contains a vertical split).
2. **File Tabs**: Currently, each split only shows one file. Support for multiple tabs per group needs to be added to the screenshot tool to make the editor look more "real-world."
3. **File Finder Results**: The File Finder modal currently lacks pre-computed results in screenshots. A potential next step is adding a way to inject "fake" matches into the YAML scenario for better visuals.
4. **Workspace Configurations**: Further exploration of different sidebar/workspace configurations to showcase the project explorer more effectively.
```

### Visual Quality Assessment: Selection Highlighting

The current selection highlighting in `sample.rs` exhibits several visual clarity and aesthetic issues:

**1. Muddled Syntax Colors**
The blue overlay is semi-transparent and desaturates the underlying syntax colors.
- **Line 11 & 18:** The yellow/orange identifier color for `name` is neutralized, resulting in a muddy, grayish-blue appearance.
- **Contrast Loss:** The overlay reduces the luminance difference between the text and the background, making the characters harder to read within the selected blocks.

**2. Visibility and Definition**
- **Low Vibrancy:** The selection color is muted and dark. While it avoids being harsh, it fails to "pop" against the dark background, making it difficult to scan the file for matches quickly.
- **Artifacting/Overlapping (Line 40):** The interaction between the multi-cursor selection and the active cursor/block creates a clashing visual. On line 40, the `name` selection is partially obscured by a brighter purple-blue block (`self.n[ame]`), leading to a disjointed look.

**3. Showcase Suitability**
- **Unappealing Palette:** The combination of the specific blue shade and the existing syntax theme results in a "dirty" color mix rather than a clean highlight.
- **Lack of Depth:** The selections lack border definition or a distinctive "glow" often found in modern editor themes designed for screenshots/showcases.

### Summary of Findings
| Aspect | Observation |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Color Fidelity** | Poor; syntax colors are significantly desaturated. |
| **Visibility** | Moderate-Low; the highlight lacks sufficient contrast from the background. |
| **Consistency** | Poor; overlapping with the primary cursor (Line 40) creates visual noise. |
| **Aesthetics** | Not recommended for showcase; the "washed out" look appears dated or unpolished. |

```
Selection highlights in the editor are rendered by filling rectangles with the `selection_background` color from the theme. The rendering logic is primarily located in `src/view/mod.rs` within both the optimized `render_cursor_lines_only` and the main editor rendering loop.

### Rendering Logic
- **Main Editor View**: The editor iterates through `editor.selections`, calculates the visual column range for each line using `char_col_to_visual_col`, and draws a solid rectangle.
- **Rectangle Selection**: A separate block handles "block" selections by calculating the intersection of the selection's visual column range with the line's content.
- **Text Fields**: Single-line inputs (like in modals or CSV editing) use a unified `TextFieldRenderer` that follows a similar pattern.

Relevant files:
- [src/view/mod.rs#L303-L354](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L303-L354) - Selection range calculation and `fill_rect_px` call for standard selections.
- [src/view/mod.rs#L356-L400](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L356-L400) - Rectangle selection rendering logic.
- [src/view/text_field.rs#L107-L139](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/text_field.rs#L107-L139) - Selection rendering for unified text fields (modals, etc).
- [src/view/frame.rs#L113-L137](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/frame.rs#L113-L137) - The `fill_rect_px` implementation that performs the actual pixel buffer writes.
```

```
The theme picker's live preview logic is implemented in `src/update/ui.rs` within the `update_ui` function, specifically handling `ModalMsg::SelectPrevious` and `ModalMsg::SelectNext`.

When navigating the list (up/down arrows), the application updates the `selected_index` of the `ThemePickerState`, retrieves the corresponding theme ID, and immediately applies it to the `model.theme` by calling `load_theme`. If the modal is closed without confirmation (`ModalMsg::Close`), the original theme is restored using the `original_theme_id` stored when the picker was opened.

Relevant files:
- [src/update/ui.rs#L190-L199](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/ui.rs#L190-L199) - Restoration of the original theme on cancel.
- [src/update/ui.rs#L608-L672](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/ui.rs#L608-L672) - Live preview logic during list navigation (SelectPrevious/Next).
- [src/update/ui.rs#L745-L759](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/ui.rs#L745-L759) - Finalizing the theme selection on confirmation.
- [src/model/ui.rs#L224-L242](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/ui.rs#L224-L242) - Definition of `ThemePickerState` including `original_theme_id`.
```

## User

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01VDhE7QwttsCoiTz5GzmfFw`

```
`EditorArea` manages nested splits using a recursive tree structure defined by the `LayoutNode` enum, which represents the workspace's layout.

### Hierarchical Tree Structure
The layout is a tree where:
- **`LayoutNode`** is the core recursive type: `Empty`, `Group(GroupId)`, `Split(SplitContainer)`, or `Preview(PreviewId)`.
- **`SplitContainer`** implements nested splits. It contains:
  - `direction`: `Horizontal` (left-to-right) or `Vertical` (top-to-bottom).
  - `children`: A `Vec<LayoutNode>`, allowing arbitrary nesting of groups or further splits.
  - `ratios`: A `Vec<f32>` defining the proportional size of each child (must sum to 1.0).
- **`EditorGroup`** is a leaf node containing a list of `Tab`s (each pointing to an `EditorState`).

### Nested Split Handling (`SplitFocused`)
When a `SplitFocused(direction)` message is handled in `src/update/layout.rs`:
1. **Creation**: `split_group` creates a new `EditorState` and `EditorGroup` for the current document.
2. **Insertion**: `insert_split_in_layout` recursively traverses the `LayoutNode` tree to find the `target_group`.
3. **Transformation**: Once found, it replaces the `Group(target_id)` node with a `Split(SplitContainer)` node containing two children: the original group and the new group.
4. **Recursive Nesting**: Because this replaces a leaf with a new split branch, users can split any pane repeatedly, creating deeply nested horizontal and vertical structures.

### Layout Calculation
The tree is rendered by `compute_layout_node` in `src/model/editor_area.rs`, which:
- Recursively divides the available `Rect` among children based on the `ratios` in the `SplitContainer`.
- Assigns the final calculated `Rect` to each `EditorGroup`.
- Returns a list of `SplitterBar` objects for hit-testing and resizing.

Relevant files:
- [src/model/editor_area.rs#L106-L183](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L106-L183) (Data structures)
- [src/update/layout.rs#L288-L391](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/layout.rs#L288-L391) (Split logic and tree insertion)
- [src/model/editor_area.rs#L718-L857](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L718-L857) (Layout computation)
- [src/update/layout.rs#L821-L980](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/layout.rs#L821-L980) (Tree traversal and splitter management)
```

All 26 scenarios render, lint clean. Here's what was done:

**Mixed/nested splits support** — Added a per-file `split` field to `ScenarioFile` in the YAML schema. Each file after the first can specify its own split direction (`horizontal` or `vertical`), falling back to the scenario-level `split_direction` default. This lets you create arbitrarily nested layouts like "horizontal split, then vertical split the right pane."

**New scenario** — `showcase-mixed-splits.yaml`: Rust (left), TypeScript (top-right), YAML (bottom-right) — demonstrating that horizontal and vertical splits can be freely combined.

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01VKDvz4VpY5JFNjJY1WeGEJ`

```
Markdown preview panes in the editor are implemented through a combination of layout management in `editor_area.rs` and a dedicated rendering system in the `src/markdown/` directory.

### Core Architecture & State
*   **PreviewId**: A unique identifier used to track preview panes within the `EditorArea` layout.
    *   [src/model/editor_area.rs#L132-L134](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L132-L134)
*   **PreviewPane**: Stores the state for a single preview, including the `DocumentId` it’s viewing, the `GroupId` it’s attached to, and the `rendered_html` content.
    *   [src/markdown/preview.rs#L5-L18](file://~/code/token-editor/src/markdown/preview.rs#L5-L18)
*   **Linking**: Previews are linked to editor groups via `EditorGroup.attached_preview`. When a preview is opened, the `EditorArea` wraps the existing group in a horizontal split to place the preview side-by-side with the editor.
    *   [src/model/editor_area.rs#L83-L91](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L83-L91) (EditorGroup definition)
    *   [src/model/editor_area.rs#L449-L479](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L449-L479) (Creation and attachment logic)

### Rendering & Scroll Sync
*   **HTML Generation**: The `markdown_to_html` function uses `pulldown-cmark` to convert markdown text into an HTML document. It injects custom CSS from `theme.rs` and a scroll-synchronization script.
    *   [src/markdown/renderer.rs#L9-L42](file://~/code/token-editor/src/markdown/renderer.rs#L9-L42)
*   **Scroll Synchronization**: Block-level elements are tagged with `data-line` attributes during parsing. A JavaScript bridge (`SCROLL_SYNC_JS`) communicates scroll positions back to the editor via `window.webkit.messageHandlers.scrollSync`.
    *   [src/markdown/renderer.rs#L288-L324](file://~/code/token-editor/src/markdown/renderer.rs#L288-L324) (JavaScript bridge)
    *   [src/markdown/renderer.rs#L327-L370](file://~/code/token-editor/src/markdown/renderer.rs#L327-L370) (Line marker injection)

### Layout Integration
*   **LayoutNode**: Previews are first-class citizens in the layout tree, alongside editor groups and split containers.
    *   [src/model/editor_area.rs#L137-L144](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L137-L144)
*   **Dynamic Layout**: Functions like `replace_group_with_split` and `remove_preview_node` handle the recursive tree transformations when previews are toggled.
    *   [src/model/editor_area.rs#L1039-L1107](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L1039-L1107)
```

## 1) TL;DR
Add an explicit **render option** to the native renderer so the screenshot binary can force **native preview rendering**, while the live app keeps using the webview overlay path unchanged. Then add a `preview: true` per-file YAML flag and open the preview for the group that owns that file after groups are created. While in there, fix a couple of correctness issues in `screenshot.rs` (notably: splitters rendered twice; CSV viewport sizing not based on actual split rects).

---

## 2) Recommended approach (simple path)

### A. Preview pane support in headless screenshots (without breaking the app)
**Goal:** For screenshots, preview panes must render their markdown content natively (the current code returns early because `USE_WEBVIEW_PREVIEW` is `true`).

**Make preview rendering a per-call option, not a global const.** Keep the existing `Renderer::render_editor_area(...)` behavior for the live app (webview mode), and add a new entry point for screenshots.

**Suggested changes in `src/view/mod.rs`:**

1) Introduce a small public “preview render mode” type:
```rust
pub enum PreviewRenderMode {
    WebviewChromeOnly,
    NativeMarkdown,
}
```

2) Keep current runtime API intact, but internally route through a new function:
```rust
pub fn render_editor_area(
    frame: &mut Frame,
    painter: &mut TextPainter,
    model: &AppModel,
    splitters: &[SplitterBar],
) {
    Self::render_editor_area_with_preview_mode(
        frame, painter, model, splitters, PreviewRenderMode::WebviewChromeOnly
    )
}

pub fn render_editor_area_with_preview_mode(
    frame: &mut Frame,
    painter: &mut TextPainter,
    model: &AppModel,
    splitters: &[SplitterBar],
    preview_mode: PreviewRenderMode,
) {
    // render groups...
    // render previews using preview_mode...
    // render splitters...
}
```

3) Update the private preview renderer to take the mode:
```rust
fn render_preview_pane(..., preview_mode: PreviewRenderMode) {
    // render chrome always
    if matches!(preview_mode, PreviewRenderMode::WebviewChromeOnly) {
        return;
    }

    // native markdown fallback (the existing code path)
}
```

4) In `src/bin/screenshot.rs`, call the new function:
```rust
Renderer::render_editor_area_with_preview_mode(
    &mut frame,
    &mut painter,
    model,
    &splitters,
    token::view::PreviewRenderMode::NativeMarkdown,
);
```

This satisfies the constraints:
- Live app stays “webview overlay owns content”
- Screenshot mode forces native markdown rendering
- No global const flip required

**Effort:** S–M (<1–3h).

---

### B. YAML schema: per-file `preview: true`
Add a boolean to `ScenarioFile`:
```rust
#[derive(Deserialize, Debug)]
struct ScenarioFile {
    path: PathBuf,
    // ...
    #[serde(default)]
    preview: bool,
}
```

Then implement an `apply_previews(model, scenario)` pass similar to `apply_view_modes`.

**Why a separate pass?**
It avoids relying on “which group is focused after split” and works even if scenario building logic changes. It also matches how CSV mode is currently applied (search by document file_path).

**Implementation sketch:**
```rust
fn apply_previews(model: &mut AppModel, scenario: &Scenario) {
    for sf in &scenario.files {
        if !sf.preview { continue; }

        // Find all groups whose active editor’s document has file_path == sf.path
        let group_ids: Vec<_> = model.editor_area.groups.iter()
            .filter_map(|(&gid, group)| {
                let editor_id = group.active_editor_id()?;
                let editor = model.editor_area.editors.get(&editor_id)?;
                let doc_id = editor.document_id?;
                let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get(&doc_id)?;
                (doc.file_path.as_ref() == Some(&sf.path)).then_some(gid)
            })
            .collect();

        for gid in group_ids {
            model.editor_area.open_preview_for_group(gid);
        }
    }
}
```

Call `apply_previews` from `create_model_from_scenario` after the split/file creation loop (and before rendering).

**Effort:** S (<1h).

---

### C. CSV + code split scenario
You said this already works, and the split/view_mode fields are in place. The only “fix” I’d strongly recommend is viewport sizing for CSV (see cleanup section), because multi-split CSV screenshots will otherwise have inconsistent row/column viewport sizes.

---

## 3) Code cleanup review of `src/bin/screenshot.rs` (high-leverage fixes)

### 1) **Splitters are rendered twice (bug)**
In `render_to_buffer` you currently do:
```rust
Renderer::render_editor_area(...);
Renderer::render_splitters(...);
```
But `Renderer::render_editor_area` **already calls** `Self::render_splitters(frame, splitters, model);` (see `src/view/mod.rs`).

**Fix:** remove the extra `Renderer::render_splitters(...)` call in `screenshot.rs`.
This is likely causing thicker/darker splitter bars in screenshots than in the real app.

### 2) **Viewport sizing isn’t synchronized to actual split rects**
`screenshot.rs` computes `visible_lines/visible_columns` once upfront based on whole window dimensions, but once you split (and/or add a workspace sidebar, and now previews), each group is smaller.

You already have `EditorArea::sync_all_viewports(line_height, char_width)` for this exact purpose.

**Fix:** in `render_to_buffer`, after `compute_layout_scaled(...)` (which updates `group.rect`), call:
```rust
model.editor_area.sync_all_viewports(font_info.line_height, font_info.char_width);
```

This will make cursors/scrolling behave more like runtime across splits.

### 3) **CSV viewport sizing should be based on each group rect (not the whole window)**
`apply_view_modes` currently computes `visible_rows` from `model.window_size`, not the group’s actual rect. With splits (and especially preview panes), this becomes wrong.

**Simplest fix that fits the current architecture:**
After layout is computed (same place as sync_all_viewports), iterate groups and if the active editor is CSV, recompute its CSV viewport size using `group.rect`.

This can live in `render_to_buffer` as a small “sync CSV viewports to layout” helper, since layout is known there.

### 4) Minor cleanup / consistency suggestions
- **Double font loading:** `create_model_from_scenario` calls `setup_font(scale)` purely for geometry, and `main` calls it again for rendering. Consider passing `(line_height, char_width)` or a `FontInfo` into `create_model_from_scenario` to avoid the duplicate work. (Not critical, but easy.)
- **Relative paths:** scenario file `path:` and `workspace.root:` are interpreted relative to current working directory, not the YAML file location. If scenarios are ever invoked from other directories, they’ll break. Consider resolving paths relative to the scenario YAML’s parent directory inside `load_scenario(...)` / `collect_scenarios(...)`. (Only do if you’re seeing pain here.)
- **Output filename safety:** `scenario.name` is used directly in the PNG filename. If names can contain spaces/slashes, you’ll get awkward paths. Consider a tiny sanitize (replace non `[a-zA-Z0-9-_]` with `_`).

**Effort for cleanup items:** M (1–3h) if you do the viewport/CSV fixes; otherwise S.

---

## 4) Risks and guardrails
- **Risk:** opening previews changes the layout tree (wraps groups in a horizontal split). If you apply previews “during” split creation, it might subtly affect subsequent split placement logic.
  **Guardrail:** apply previews in a separate `apply_previews(...)` pass after all splits/groups are created (recommended above).
- **Risk:** new renderer entry point could accidentally diverge from runtime if it doesn’t share code.
  **Guardrail:** keep `render_editor_area(...)` as the canonical runtime API and implement the new function as a thin wrapper around the same logic.

---

## 5) When to consider the advanced path
Only consider a deeper refactor if:
- You want the native preview to support much richer markdown (wrapping, inline styling, tables), or
- You need pixel-perfect parity between webview and native preview.

At that point you’d likely want to actually use the existing `pulldown-cmark` pipeline to produce `rendered_lines: Vec<RenderedLine>` and render those segments natively (instead of the current “simple line heuristics”), but that’s beyond what’s needed for screenshots right now.

---

## 6) Optional advanced path (brief)
Add a real “native preview renderer” module that:
- parses markdown via `pulldown-cmark`,
- produces `PreviewPane.rendered_lines` (StyledSegments),
- uses theme styles to render segments (including wrapping),
- and is invoked when `PreviewRenderMode::NativeMarkdown`.

This would make screenshots much closer to webview output, but it’s a larger change (L/XL).
```

## Assistant

All 37 tests pass, lint clean, all 29 scenarios render. Here's a summary of everything done:

**New features in the screenshot tool:**
- **Preview pane support** — Added `preview: bool` per-file YAML field. When true, opens a native markdown preview pane beside the editor group. Introduced `PreviewRenderMode` enum in `src/view/mod.rs` so screenshots use native markdown rendering while the live app continues using webview.
- **Per-file split direction** (from prior thread) — Each file can specify `split: horizontal` or `split: vertical` independently.

**Bug fixes (caught by oracle review):**
- **Double splitter rendering** — `render_to_buffer` was calling `render_splitters` separately, but `render_editor_area` already calls it internally. Removed the duplicate call.
- **Viewport sync** — Added `sync_all_viewports()` call after layout computation so each editor's viewport matches its actual group rect size (critical for split/preview layouts).

**New scenarios created:**
- `showcase-csv-split.yaml` — CSV spreadsheet + Rust code side by side
- `showcase-markdown-preview.yaml` — Markdown source + native preview pane
- `showcase-html-preview.yaml` — HTML+preview on top, JavaScript below (nested splits)

**Showcase gallery updated** — 3 new entries added to `showcase.ts`, 'Preview' tag added to the filter bar.

## User

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01R3sA4846e5ozve7iaTAbJ5`

```
### Layout Description
The layout utilizes a recursive binary splitting pattern. Starting with a vertical split, each subsequent "remaining" pane is divided into two, alternating between horizontal and vertical axes. This creates a fractal-like nested structure where each level reduces the viewport area by half, resulting in a visual spiral that converges toward the bottom-right corner.

### Pane Count and Visual Pattern
There are **11 visible panes**. The alternating splits create an interesting geometric progression, effectively demonstrating the editor's ability to handle deep nesting, though the text becomes unreadable by the 9th level.

### Visible Languages/Files
1.  **Rust** (`sample.rs` - Left pane)
2.  **TypeScript** (`sample.ts` - Top-right pane)
3.  **Python** (`sample.py` - Middle-left of bottom-right section)
4.  **Go** (`sample.go` - Top-right of remaining section)
5.  **Java** (`sample.java` - Middle-left of remaining section)
6.  **C** (`sample.c` - Top-right of remaining section)
7.  **HTML** (`sample.html` - Middle-left of remaining section)
8.  **CSS** (`sample.css` - Top-right of remaining section)
9.  **JSON** (`sample.json` - Middle-left of remaining section)
10. **YAML** (`sample.yaml` - Top-right of last section)
11. **Markdown** (`sample.md` - Bottom-right of last section)
```

## User

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01KGecbV39XgEaFqL7r4miiX`

```
When a new split is created via `SplitFocused`, the split ratios are established in the `insert_split_in_layout` helper function in `src/update/layout.rs`.

### Default Ratio Establishment
The 50/50 ratio is hardcoded as `vec![0.5, 0.5]` when a `LayoutNode::Group` is replaced by a new `LayoutNode::Split`.

- [src/update/layout.rs#L366-L376](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/layout.rs#L366-L376): The `insert_split_in_layout` function handles the tree transformation and sets the initial ratios.
- [src/model/editor_area.rs#L1047-L1052](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L1047-L1052): A similar 50/50 ratio is established when a group is replaced with a split containing a preview pane.

### Custom Ratio API
There is currently no high-level public API to set custom ratios on a `SplitContainer`. Instead, the ratios are managed through:
1. **Initial Creation**: Hardcoded to 0.5/0.5 in the layout update functions.
2. **Interactive Resizing**: The `update_container_ratios_by_splitter` function provides a way to adjust existing ratios during a drag operation.
3. **Internal Helpers**: `remove_preview_node` (in `src/model/editor_area.rs#L1091-L1095`) re-calculates equal ratios when a child is removed.

Relevant files:
- [src/update/layout.rs#L33-L36](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/layout.rs#L33-L36) - Entry point for `SplitFocused` message.
- [src/update/layout.rs#L368-L376](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/layout.rs#L368-L376) - Implementation of 50/50 split creation.
- [src/update/layout.rs#L943-L962](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/layout.rs#L943-L962) - Internal ratio update mechanism.
- [src/model/editor_area.rs#L120-L130](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L120-L130) - `SplitContainer` struct definition.
```

### Width Analysis

The first vertical divider is positioned at approximately the **25% mark** from the left edge.
- The **left pane** (`sample.rs`) occupies roughly one-quarter of the total image width.
- The **right area** (containing `sample.ts`, `sample.py`, `sample.go`, etc.) occupies the remaining three-quarters (75%).

### Visual Balance Comparison

*   **25/75 Split (Current):** This allocation favors the recursive nesting on the right. It provides enough horizontal space for the deep fractal structure to remain legible, while the Rust code on the left—which has relatively short line lengths—does not suffer from excessive empty margin.
*   **50/50 Split (Hypothetical):** A 50/50 split would move the divider to the center of the screen. This would result in significant wasted horizontal space in the `sample.rs` pane and severely compress the complex grid on the right, likely causing text truncation and making the smaller bottom-right panes (like `sample.html`, `sample.css`) unreadable.

All 30 scenarios render, lint clean. Added `split_ratio` support to the YAML schema — any file can now specify its proportion (0.0–1.0) in the parent split. The fractal screenshot now has Rust at 25% with the recursive spiral filling the remaining 75%.
